# Global Strategy

# Quantamental Signal Ideas - Top & Bottom Ranked Stocks

Details on the methodology/model can be found in our Q-Series report

## What are the model’s recommendations today?

Based on the stock-level scores and an optimisation for portfolio balance, we find that the highest-scoring stocks tend to come from sectors including Energy, Banks, Communication Services and Cap Goods. The lowest-scoring stock list is dominated by Household Personal Products, Semis, Food Beverage & Tobacco, Staples, and Health Care stocks. Do note these recommendations can differ from the fundamental views of our equity strategy team.

## Digging into the details, what key message is each signal cohort suggesting?

• Price momentum keeps improving for Chinese stocks. Info Tech and Comm. Services momentums have started to fade recently, although still positive. Overall, the most favoured sectors are Energy, Financials & Materials, the least favoured are Real Estate, Comm. Services & Health Care.

• Positioning remains very supportive in China, while being more balanced in other regions. In the US and Europe, Real estate is clearly the sector to overweight based on positioning. In the other regions, the top pick is consistently Info Tech. Positioning in Health Care stocks has significantly improved vs. March, as investors may start to feel a bit nervous/more defensive after a strong start of the year.

• Earnings expectations and momentum seems to favour Real Estate (ex China) and to a lesser extent, Financials stocks. On the opposite side, the more defensive sectors are the least favoured. Energy saw the biggest drop, especially in Canada, Australia and Japan. • With OECD G20 Composite Leading index indicating expansion as the most likely regime in 2024 (43% probability, vs. 12% for a downturn, and 3% higher than previous update), cyclical sectors continue to be favoured in our regime model.

• On valuation, Financials & Utilities remain the cheapest sectors in our adjusted 12mth fwd PE model, when Tech & Health Care remain the most expensive. Since our last update, relative valuations have slightly improved in Consumer Discretionary & Financials, and slightly worsened in Utilities & Materials.

• Sector PMI new orders indicate a significant rotation across sectors. Health Care, which was ranking worst in March, is now ranking best. Staples did the opposite move. Rankings appear to be consistent across regions.

## What are the industries whose rankings have changed the most?

Our model is raising its (already high) risk-on stance, reverting its start of the year risk reduction. 21 out of 24 industries have seen their overall scores upgraded, mostly driven by improving sector PMIs and positive price momentum globally. Household Personal Products, Materials, Utilities and Auto components are the ones exhibiting the highest improvements. Only Consumer services experienced a significant drop in its overall scoring.

<table><tr><td colspan="2">Quick reminder of the 11 quantamental signals</td></tr><tr><td>i)</td><td>(Four) CTA Momentum Signals: aim to benefit from the persistence in asset prices. Our CTA trend-following algorithm is applied on 1) stock, 2) industry, 3) sector and 4) country index prices</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="2">ii)</td><td>(Three) Regime Probability Signals: rely on the principle that assets&#x27; prices are greatly influenced by economic regimes.</td></tr><tr><td>Estimating each regime probability and positioning accordingly can be a great source of alpha</td></tr><tr><td>iii)</td><td>Crowding Signal: empirical work suggests we should participate during the building phase of a &#x27;consensus&#x27; trade and only be contrarian - i.e. play short-term mean reversion - when positioning has become extreme</td></tr><tr><td>iv)</td><td>EPS Growth (ML vs Consensus) Signal: attempt to identify stocks with the highest risk of upgrades/downgrades from analysts. Surprises are powerful drivers of absolute and relative returns</td></tr><tr><td>v)</td><td>(Extreme) Rates Momentum Signal: speed and magnitude of bond moves are among the key factors of investors&#x27; risk appetite. Increasing equity risk after a strong rally or sell-off in rates has historically been rewarded</td></tr><tr><td>vi)</td><td>Risk Targeting Signal: risk targeting is an &#x27;optimized&#x27; long-only strategy that relies on two levers - volatilities and correlations - to produce superior returns</td></tr><tr><td>vii) viii)</td><td>Up&amp;Down Volatility Signal: is a price-based indicator of positioning that gauges the probability of a trend reversal</td></tr><tr><td></td><td>Options Volumes Signal: is a leading indicator of investors&#x27; nervousness. Some investors seek to postpone the unpleasant feeling of crystallizing losses using derivatives hedging. More often than not, it leads to capitulation</td></tr><tr><td>ix)</td><td>Adjusted Forward PE Ratio Signal: value is a popular strategy for patient investors. We enhance one of the most popular valuation indictors, the fPE Ratio, by adjusting the numerator (i.e. prices) by the quality of the companies&#x27; balance sheets</td></tr><tr><td>x)</td><td>Sector PMI (New Orders) Signal: relies on the same economic priors as the Regime Probability signal. The main difference is that rather than a single leading index, we utilise sector level data on a specific leading indicator that surveys businesses directly on their forward looking business activity</td></tr><tr><td>xi)</td><td>Earnings Revisions Signal: profits from revisions in analysts&#x27; earnings estimates. Even small changes in expectations can have a big impact on a stock&#x27;s price</td></tr></table>

## What our US (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/ lowest ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Communication Services

John Hodulik on Walt Disney Co: We have a Buy rating on Disney. We expect continued growth at the parks, driven by a rapid expansion of capacity in the high margin cruise segment. We also expect the streaming business to generate double-digit margins over the next 2 years while sports begins to grow once the NBA renewal and costs associated with the launch of flagship streaming ESPN are absorbed.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Communication Services

John Hodulik on Warner Bros Discovery: Warner Bros is a Neutral but we expect it to lag given continued pressure in linear TV with an acceleration in cord cutting and weak advertising market. The company faces the choice of overpaying to renew NBA rights or losing those rights and seeing increased pressure on distribution and advertising revenue streams.

John Hodulik on Charter Communications Inc: Charter is also a Neutral rated stock. It is seeing increased pressure on broadband subscriber volumes given competition from fixed and wireless substitutes. Losses will likely accelerate over the next 2 quarters as the Affordable Connectivity Program winds down. Charter had 5M over its over 30M subscribers on the program and should see increased churn as a result. We expect that to pressure EBITDA growth in 2Q and 3Q of this year.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Joseph Spak on General Motors Co: We have a Buy rating on GM. We think the company remains a top autos idea given 1) Likely positive earnings revision driven by pricing not being as negative as was assumed in plan and strong traction on cost. 2) Consistent buyback potential. Likely to repurchase \$0.8bn above ASR (\$10bn) and see potential for additional buyback authorization/ASR of \~\$7bn later this year. 3) Valuation is not onerous, in our view.

Jay Sole on Ralph Lauren Corp: We have a Buy rating on RL. We think the market doesn't appreciate the transformational changes RL has made to its brand image, distribution model, and cost structure. We forecast a 13% 5-yr. EPS CAGR.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Jay Sole on Ross Stores Inc: We are Neutral on Ross. We see Off-Price retailers like ROST as secular outperformers as they offer better value to consumers than their rivals do. This likely leads to market share gains versus struggling channels such as Department Stores. We model a 7% 5-year EPS CAGR (FY23-FY28E).

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Faham Baig on Altria Group Inc: We have a Sell rating on Altria. The group is facing headwinds from a more challenged US market (100% of EBIT), where it is investing behind lower-priced Marlboro Black, and should result in Smokeable sales and OCI declining in FY24. The company is also attempting to catch up in next-generation products (NGP), which will prove difficult, in our view, resulting in Altria's share of the nicotine category declining. Whilst the recently announced buyback has resulted in the shares bouncing, we do not believe it is sustainable.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Energy

Josh Silverstein on Exxon Mobil Corp: We have a Buy rating on Exxon. Our positive outlook is driven by visible upstream growth, new downstream capacity additions, cost reductions, and balance sheet strength.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Erika Najarian on Citizens Financial Group: We have a Buy on Citizens. The stock remains at a discount to peers on both P/TBV and P/E despite strong capital levels, positive early results from the buildout of its Private Bank, and above average deposit growth expectations. We think CFG’s net interest income (NII) trajectory is less dependent on the macro and more on execution as a result, and the launch point for CFG’s ’25 NII looks solid ahead of reduced headwinds from out of the money swaps and non-core assets next year.

Erika Najarian on PNC Financial Services Group: We are neutral PNC. While net interest income looks set to inflect in 2024 ahead of an expected record year in 2025 and interest rate sensitivity is largely neutral, we think the stock’s premium to peers on both P/TBV and P/E multiples fairly values shares, especially as the current rate backdrop is more favorable for those with greater asset sensitivity and more attractive valuations.

Brian Meredith on Allstate Corp: We believe ALL has excellent margin improvement and earnings visibility as price increase taken over the last year earn into results while loss cost inflation moderates. We also see the opportunity for the shares re-rating as the company pivots to growth towards the end of 2024 aided by initiatives put in place to drive growth over the last several years. We remain a Buy on the shares and it remains a top pick.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Nik Cremo on Jack Henry & Associates Inc: We are Neutral rated on JKHY and don’t think it makes sense to flag as sell on this list. The business has a high valuation but is very stable/defensive given LT contracts/98-99% customer retention.

Brennan Hawken on Franklin Resources Inc: We are Neutral on the Stock. Franklin has experienced elevated outflows, as investors continue to rotate out of higher cost, active products, favoring lower fee passive options. Franklin’s recent acquisitions of alternative asset managers have helped to stabilize the fee rate, but the flows into their alts products have skewed a bit weaker, which will make it hard from them to offset the traditional outflows. Also, the expense cuts from the recent Putnam acquisition, were more back-end weighted than many investors has expected.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Dan Leonard on Charles River Laboratories: Our Buy rating on CRL reflects our view that the company can sustain HSD+ organic sales growth while achieving modest margin expansion. Recent Q1 results and reaffirmed FY2024 guidance have mixed implications for our thesis. While CRL’s Manufacturing (MFS) and Research Models and Services business (RMS) have recently outperformed expectations, CRL’s core business, Discovery and Safety Assessment (DSA), has performed relatively weakly - declining y/y and with a book-to-bill ratio of \~.83x. That said, trends in DSA have begun to appear healthier as funding for the underlying biopharma end market has increased with improving y/y and q/q Safety Assessment proposal activity.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Dan Leonard on West Pharmaceutical Services: Our Neutral rating on WST reflects our view that destocking headwinds, margin pressures, and capacity constraints at the company’s facilities are likely to limit upside in the near-term. The recent Q1 beat was partially driven by favorable order timing, and FY2024 guidance was maintained; destocking has not fully concluded and is expected to persist into Q2, but WST is expecting sequential sales growth. Margins were weak in Q1’24, but are expected to improve progressively throughout FY2024 alongside a recovery in sales. Over the medium term, increasing regulation, such as Annex-1 in Europe, should increase WST’s sales of high-value products (HVPs) and help drive modest margin expansion.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Gavin Parsons on Lockheed Martin Corp: We are Neutral on LMT. It is the largest Defense company in the world and a good proxy for national security expenditures, which we see increasing globally. That said, we see flattish free cash flow for the next few years as LMT works through pension headwinds and F-35 delays.

Steven Fisher on Paccar Inc: We have a Buy rating on Paccar. We think there’s upside to consensus numbers on expectations for a truck upcycle in 2025-2026, even as we start a down year in 2024. We also think there are underappreciated company specific factors for PCAR that will drive margins ahead of consensus.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Kevin McVeigh on Paychex Ind: We are Neutral on Paychex because 1) Durable growth + FCF generation—constructive longer-term despite macro uncertainty as we believe stock isn’t discounting enough macro concern, 2) Stock has been option for yield investors given durable FCF, leading margins, amid \~3% dividend yield, 3) Paychex is levered to SMBs, which could be challenging in 2024-25 as it anniversaries downmarket hiring which absorbed upmarket slack, and 4) \$120 PT PAYX is 24x 2026E EPS ex-cash—compared to 3-yr range of 23.2x-35.2x + average of 28.7x. Price target based on 2 year multiple on CY26 estimates, rolled forward by Cost of Equity to derive 1 year price target.

Gavin Parsons on General Dynamics Corp: We are Neutral on the stock. General Dynamics is benefitting from the increase in global military spend with exposure to both short-cycle (ammunition) and long-cycle (submarines), and the recently introduced G700 is driving growth in Gulfstream. That said, we believe the business jet market is oversupplied and see pressure on business jet bookings, production levels, and cash in the coming years.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

David Vogt on Arista Networkds Inc: Arista is a leading provider of Ethernet switches deployed across hyperscaler and enterprise data centers. Over the past several years, revenue growth has been strong as customer invested significant capital into expanding data centers as workloads moved to the cloud. Going forward, investments in AI infrastructure should support mid-teens revenue growth in CY24 and CY25. However, with the shares trading at almost 40x NTM P/E, we believe the market is already discounting/expecting strong revenue and EPS growth over the next several years. Therefore, we rate the shares Neutral.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

Timothy Arcuri on Seagate Technology Holdings: We are Neutral on Seagate. The HAMR story has sputtered a bit but appears back on track and should add some idiosyncratic cost scaling for STX over time, and while we are bullish on pricing for STX, we do believe that SSDs are capturing a much bigger portion of incremental demand around AI where performance is paramount, and we worry this caps STX's volume growth this next cycle.

David Vogt on Intl Business Machines (IBM): IBM’s historical growth offerings, Red Hat and Consulting, are likely to remain soft over the next several years as customers increasingly allocate investment and capital to more AI centric hyperscaler solutions. Therefore, the company is increasingly reliant on expensive and in some cases dilutive M&A (Apptio, HashiCorp pending) to offset slower growth in the core business. Finally, the company has regularly used non-operating one-time items to grow EPS and cash flow that is not sustainable in our view. Therefore, we rate the shares Sell with a \$130 price target, or over 20% downside from the current share price.

Timothy Arcuri on Intel Corp: We are Neutral on Intel. Gross margin looks set to inflect significantly in 2H and the stock has tended to follow gross margin, and estimates have certainly been derisked ahead of a broader cycle recovery, but we still struggle here on the big picture as the company is removing off-ramps for the product group that were added the past 3yrs, seemingly on high expectations of manufacturing success - where our checks on 18A remain mixed. 14A (high NA) should be a much more robust and competitive process but this is not going to be until 2029/2030 - far too long to wait while the product roadmap atrophies.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Avalonbay Communities Inc: We have a Neutral rating on AvalonBay. The company’s Coastal portfolio should continue to deliver same store growth in the top end of the peer group given favorable supply/demand dynamics. Normalizing bad debt should be a tailwind for AVB in 2024. Additionally, we expect strong value creation from the development pipeline with projects delivering into a favorable supply environment.

Michael Goldsmith on Invitation Homes Inc: We have a Buy rating on Invitation Homes. We believe INVH is well-positioned heading into the peak leasing season with elevated occupancy. While new lease growth was soft in 1Q’24, growth rates accelerated in April which we think is an indication of success from INVH’s occupancy strategy. Further, demand for SFRs remains strong with US housing undersupplied by over 2m units. Normalizing bad debt should be a tailwind for INVH in 2024.

Michael Goldsmith on Essex Property Trust Inc: We have a Neutral rating on Essex. There have been signs of a recovery in ESS’ West Coast markets. For example, tech job openings have increased and return-to-office policies have been bringing employees back to markets like Seattle’s East Side. Normalizing bad debt should be a tailwind for ESS in 2024. We believe ESS has conservatively accounted for watchlist properties in its preferred equity program.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Real Estate

Michael Goldsmith on Mid America Apartment : We have a Sell rating on MAA. Demand has held up well for Sunbelt Apartment properties, but supply has limited pricing power. For example, new lease spreads are typically positive and trending higher by April. In April 2024, MAA’s new lease spreads were down -6.1%, which was just a 10 bps improvement over 1Q’24. Supply should peak in 2H’24 but remain impactful through 2025.

Alex Kramm on CBRE Group: We are Neutral on CBRE. We view the company as the blue chip in the CRE brokerage space. The company has diversified well into more recurring outsourcing businesses, which should have stable and attractive growth profiles. From a cyclical perspective, the stock should benefit from a thawing of capital markets activity and a more active leasing market.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

Gregg Orrill on Eversource: (Buy, \$71 PT). ES' 20% discount valuation to the Utility average on our '25 \$4.87 EPS forecast ignores the renewed focus on the core delivery business and debt reduction. Instead the stock prices in a 50 bp reduction to our EPS growth forecast (4.5% vs. 5.0%) while ignoring upside cap-ex opportunities which could total \$1 billion and represent 0.5% EPS growth upside. Three events help ES improve FFO/debt 200 bp to 14% and support 5% EPS growth with progress toward the 6% industry average: Announcing a buyer for the Aquarion water utility; closing the \$1.1 billion sale for ES' 50% ownership in the South Fork and Revolution offshore wind projects to GIP; and the closing sale of the Sunrise offshore wind project in New York.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

Gregg Orrill on American Water Works: We are Neutral on AWK. The stock trades at a 7% premium to the Water Utility average (AWK, WTRG, AWR, CWT, SJW) based on our \$5.63 ’25 EPS forecast despite an outlook for average EPS growth. While the

Pennsylvania rate case has a draft decision and is mostly complete, the proposed outcome represents a reduction in allowed ROE 9.6% versus 10.0% currently. There also remains near-term uncertainty regarding the Pennsylvania PUC review of rules on rate treatment of acquisition premiums.

## What our European (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Adam Berlin on Universal Music Group: We have a Buy rating on UMG. We believe a combination of momentum in paid streaming (penetration and price rises), cost savings and limited further spend on catalogue acquisitions means UMG can deliver strong FCF growth to >€2bn by 2025E, and 11% ahead of consensus. In 2024E, we forecast 12% growth in UMG organic streaming revenues (vs VA cons 11%). On our estimates the group trades on an attractive 4.1% FY25 FCF yield.

Adam Berlin on Publicis Group: We have a Buy rating on Publicis. Our 2025E EPS is 4% ahead of consensus. While a buyback of above €200m would have been an incremental positive, we expect M&A to help Publicis deliver an 8% 2024-26E EPS CAGR. Along with the larger dividend, this implies an annual TSR of 12% even if shares do not continue to re-rate. But we think a further re-rating is still possible with shares trading at 12x.

Ondrej Cabejsek on Telenor Asa: Buy; PT 150) has simplified its footprint significantly with several exits/mergers in Asia at the same time that its Nordics business has started growing at one of the highest rates in the industry. Supported by structural opex contraction from legacy network decommissioning and power purchase agreements, Telenor looks set to continue growing EBITDA by MSD over the next couple of years while its capex/sales ratio is falling at the fastest pace in the industry on the back of FTTH completion and supportive regulation in Norway, from 19% to 13-14% by 2025. With consolidation on the associate level, FCF from Asia is guided to double between 2023 to 2025. As a result, Telenor will cover its 7.5% dividend yield next year, at 2.2x ND/EBITDA, with consensus being materially behind this guidance and we therefore see space for expectation upgrades as the company continues to deliver on its targets.

Polo Tang on Deutsche Telekom: We see Deutsche Telekom as a cheap quality defensive offering attractive shareholder returns and well positioned in two attractive markets (US/ Germany). It is trading on 12x PE and offers >10% pa EPS growth with 6% shareholder returns (o/w the dividend is 4% pa and growing double-digits with a 2% pa share buyback). Shares have been impacted near-term by concerns over a government placing but stepping stones to drive a re-rating include clarity on Germany spectrum, German wage inflation (June) and shareholder returns/use of cash at the CMD (October). See our recent detailed note (link).

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Zuzanna Pusz on Hermes: RMS continues to be seen as the luxury sector's most resilient, unique supply-driven equity story, with demand for its leather goods division (\~41% of sales in 2023) exceeding supply. As such we expect 2024 to be a year when the company could consolidate further its position as one of the sector's most defensive stocks amid an industry-wide slowdown with our estimated +14% OSG vs. +5% for peers given more pricing and volume resilience, and very limited EBIT margin pressure (-50bp y/y driven primarily by non-recurrence of hedging gains). RMS remains our top-pick. (for more, see Hermès International SCA "2024 Outlook: Defying gravity").

Zuzanna Pusz on adidas: After some turbulent years of company-specific and industrywide issues, ADS now appears to be on track to regain its lost market share, driven by sector-wide tailwinds an accelerating brand momentum (up +5% underlying in 1Q24). With signs of sustained product innovation, positive trends for key Originals footwear franchises and an increased focus on wholesale driving outperformance versus its peers, we expect +11% c.FX sales growth in both 2024 and 2025 and an EBIT margin expansion to 4.5% and 7.8% in 2024 and 2025 given reduced discounting and contained marketing spend (for more, see adidas AG "1Q results increase our confidence in the story").

Ivar Billfalk-Kelly on Compass Group: Compass Group is the best in class contract caterer, in our view, given its track record of delivering higher organic growth combined with a higher and more stable margin than its closest peers, a position we expect it to solidify. We expect the group to capitalise on the current inflection point in the market of faster revenue and earnings growth driven by: 1) an acceleration in first time outsourcing, which could add 2.5ppts of revenue growth to the market over the coming decade if penetration rates reach levels seen for cleaning or security, and 2) the group is best placed to take advantage of future M&A opportunities to fill gaps in its offering but in the absence of M&A opportunities we see scope for continued share buy backs owing to strong cash flow and low leverage. The group's prospects for growth are better than at its peers, in our view, justifying a premium valuation. We rate the shares to Buy.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Ben Shelley on Entain Plc: We are Neutral on the stock. We believe Entain represents good-value access to a structurally growing online market, however, owing to a more muted immediate catalyst pathway, we are more reserved at this juncture. While we think Entain is a collection of mostly attractive businesses, evidenced by strong customer growth and predominantly top 3 market positions, the group faces a mostly negative near-term trajectory, with declining revenues and receding market share across a number of its territories. We believe this leaves investors reluctant to buy into Entain's growth story despite its now undemanding valuation. In order to re-rate, we think Entain needs to deliver on an inflection in both its revenue and competitive trajectory, which looks signposted to come as we exit 2024. We wait for more encouraging data points in BetMGM and the online business before having more conviction on any potential inflection.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Henri Patricot on Orlen SA: We are Neutral on Orlen. It is the largest energy company in Poland. While the extensive investment plan over the next \~5 years provides for a negative FCF yield on our assumptions, the group has low leverage at this stage and reiterated its commitment to the base dividend which should yield 6.2% for FY24E and 6.5% for FY25E. We are cautiously optimistic on the recent management change for the group but consider that for a meaningful re-rating of Orlen shares we would need to see an update on the group’s strategy and projects planned.

Henri Patricot on Repsol SA: We rate Repsol Buy, as we see the company as likely to surprise positively on cashflow generation which should allow it to maintain high shareholder returns and transition towards low-carbon faster than peers. We expect the company to continue raising the dividends and maintain buybacks at >€1bn p.a., providing a sector-leading distribution yield of \~14% over 2024-25E.

Henri Patricot on ENI SPA: We rate the stock Buy. Eni has traded at a 20% EV/DACF average discount to peers over the past five years, reflecting a weaker performance on returns driven by cost overruns and a bias towards a weak Italian downstream market. The stock now trades in line with peers, but we think a premium is now warranted. A new development approach has reduced the risk of project delays in the upstream, natural gas trading margins are supported by restructuring, the transition businesses are gaining scale, and the refining business is being replaced by a higher-returning biofuels offering.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Michal Potyra on Powszechny Zaklad Ubezpiecze: We are Neutral on PZU. The company looks once again in the value territory. Following the strong performance of the PZUcontrolled banks, the insurance ‘stub’ looks cheap => we calculate the core insurance P/ E at only 8x now, down from 11x in December. Dividend announcement in the high-end of the guidance (PLN 4.34/share) further supports the investment case.

Adele Palama on Poste Italiane Spa: We rate PT a Buy. We are more constructive on the ability of Poste to report a healthy 24-28E earning progression and resilient EBIT margin though we remain more conservative than management on 24-28E revenues targets. The stock is trading at c8x EPS25E from consensus, a discount versus historical levels and selected peers while offering an attractive dividend yield of c8% with 65% payout. A sustained capital growth expected (c7% p.a.) will pave the way for a potential higher dividend, with Poste having the flexibility to raise its payout up to 70-80%, we believe, leading to almost 10% capital return yield.

Michael Werner on Amundi SA: We have a Buy rating on the stock. We think Amundi is well positioned to benefit from fixed income funds inflows in 2024 (we started to see positive flows into active fixed income in late 2023) as well as allocations to passive strategies. Amundi fund investment performance has improved over the past 12-18 months which has positioned the company well to benefit from these flow trends.

Nasib Ahmed on Legal & General Group: We are Neutral on the stock. L&G has a capital markets day scheduled for 12 June, when we expect a marginally positive update from the new CEO. Focus will likely shift towards capital-light business, greater clarity on capital management and a simplified business model. See our expectations in our note here: Legal & General Group PLC "Into the unknown: Delving into L&G's future plans"

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Justin Forsythe on Worldline SA: We have a Sell rating on Worldline. We think numbers for 2024 are not fully derisked, and on a cash flow basis the valuation appears more reasonable. Also we would see a full fledged take out as upside risk, however there could be a French solution / minority stake / backing by existing partners, less likely to result in M&A related upside compared to Nexi in our view. Upside case is they just hit numbers and operational risks don’t materialise with 200m restructuring plans.

Michael Werner on London Stock Exchange Group: We rate LSEG Neutral. While we view the long-term opportunities for LSEG attractively, particularly on a 3-5 year time horizon, we expect a lack of positive catalysts for the remainder of the year. Given LSEG's strong multiple expansion since the start of 2023 (accounting for c85% of the 30% upside), we think we need to see upgrades to consensus EPS before we see any further multiple expansion.

Nasib Ahmed on Ageas: We have a Sell rating on the stock. Ageas’ free cash flow generation relative to the dividend cost looks low, with one-off cash generation supplementing the holding company cash position. We also have China macro concerns, which should weigh on the stock in the near term.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Health Care

Xian Deng on Ipsen: We have a Buy rating on Ipsen. It is a midcap pharma company that grows at above sector rate but trades at a discount. Ipsen is one of the least binary stocks in our SMID pharma/biotech coverage as the growth is largely driven by approved assets. We like Dysport (Ipsen’s botox equivalent for cosmetics use), and we believe its consumer characteristics could drive sustained long term growth. The longevity is often underappreciated and it is what makes Dysport the biggest asset by NPV in our model.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Health Care

Graham Doyle on Coloplast: We are Neutral on Coloplast. It is a high quality manufacturer of chronic care products which is leveraging its dominant European market positions to drive growth in the US and to a lesser extent emerging markets. That said, the business is maturing in our view making organic growth targets harder to achieve and expectations remain high.

Matthew Weston on Sartorius Stdeim Biotech: We are Neutral on the stock. While we expect Sartorius Stedim to benefit from a pickup in bioprocess consumable volumes demand over 2H24/25, we remain more cautious than consensus on the mid-term growth and margin potential at SSB. We assume high industry bioprocess consumables capacity post-COVID will drive lower price momentum and cost pressures mid-term.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Rory Smith on SKF AB: SKF shares shrugged off recent weak PMIs, which might be an indication that investors are now more willing to engage in the self-help story for this once perceived PMI-proxy stock. Q1 profits beat expectations despite slightly weaker top line, supporting this central pillar to our Buy thesis.

Nicole Manion on ISS A/S: We have a Buy rating on ISS. We see an attractive valuation for an ongoing recovery story, with increasing confidence in the margin to return to >5% (UBSe 5.01% margin in FY'27e) driven by successful portfolio transformation resulting in improved FCF generation (other than the withheld payment from the DT contract). Organic momentum has sustained for the past two years driven by a better pricing muscle with improved contracting, strong volumes and high customer retention.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Nicole Manion on Rentokil Initial Plc: We have a Sell rating on the stock. While management have already now pushed out targets for US Pest Control and see 2024 as a ‘transition year’, we still think that growth debates will continue into 2025+ given headwinds from strong competition, and unknowns around branch integration and pay harmonisation. We also think that consensus underestimates the investments required to overcome this, and see a continued period of lower earnings quality ahead (due to e.g. exceptional costs and termite provisions) which may weigh on valuation.

Rory McKenzie on Ashtead Group Plc: (Neutral, PT 5,850p) - We remain positive on Sunbelt US’s long-term structural opportunity, with the group launching its 5-year strategy 'Sunbelt 4.0' at the Apr-24 CMD (see note here), targeting a +10% EPS organic CAGR. However, markets today look challenging with still-slowing rental demand given tightening impacts on US construction spending, and the recent catch-up in rental supply; we expect limited organic volume growth this year and see some risks to the rental rate outlook.

Marcus Cole on Travis Perkins Plc: We are Neutral on the stock. Prospects remain challenging given subdued UK residential markets and the group is in cash conservation mode to reduce leverage from 2.6x in FY23 towards 1.5-2.0x target. Management is working on a potential exit from Toolstation France (UBSE -£20m EBITA in FY24) and is conducting a strategic review of Toolstation Benelux (UBSE -£12m EBITA in FY24). Exiting Toolstation Europe would clearly be earnings accretive in the short-term (+25% to FY24 pro-forma EPS) but it was seen as a material growth driver over the mediumterm. Valuation prices in a reasonable level of earnings recovery at \~19.5x 2024E P/E (LT avg: 11.5x), which we think reflects the potential exit from Toolstation Europe but also already bakes in a reasonable level of end market recovery.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Francois-Xavier Bouvignies on AMS Osram AG: We are Neutral on AMS. We expect benefits from cost savings toward the end of the year especially driven by a cycle recovery in auto and smartphone markets.

Rory Smith on Spectris: We are Neutral on Spectris. It appears to be transpiring that Life Science consumable plays are recovering from destocking while equipment names like Spectris have more challenges to growth. We are also cautious of Auto’s capex for the Dynamics division; but with strong net cash, Spectris might be able to grow out of any weaker markets this year.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Priyanka Patel on K+S AG: We are Sell rated on K+S given it is a high cost producer of potash (70% of current production in Germany) and short term we see limited recovery in the potash price, and therefore earnings & FCF, with unsupportive farmer economics. K+S also faces longer-term earnings risk due to increasing potash supply from BHP (2026).

Daniel Major on Boliden AB: We are Sell rated on BOL as we see earnings pressure from smelters due to lower zinc/copper TCRC’s & mines due to downside risk to zinc prices and we believe this will outweigh tailwinds from copper. Near-term BOL has high capex, -ve FCF and rising net debt and in our view upside risk to 2025 capex may put pressure on capex medium-term.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Charles Boissier on Warehouses De Pauw: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Operations continue to benefit from the mega trends in e-commerce, re-shoring and supply chain resilience. More structurally, logistics real estate space per capita in Europe is still a fraction of the more mature US market, hence market rents are still rising in Europe, making Continental European Logistics one of our strongest convictions within European real estate.

Tommaso Operto on Swiss Prime Site: We have a Buy rating on SPS. We see the company as a main beneficiary from reviving real estate transaction volumes through its asset management arm. Despite its operationally streamlined business model, the stock continues to be relatively underowned, and we believe that at the current share discount to NAV, the asset management business is effectively valued at zero.

Zachary Gauge on British Land Co: We have a Buy rating on the stock. British Land’s portfolio looks well positioned to benefit from the polarization in occupational demand for best-in-class Central London office space, as evidenced by the recent 250,000 sq ft leasing to Citadel at 2 Finsbury Avenue. Their sector tilts towards the knowledge based economy and life sciences enable them to benefit from the strong macro drivers behind this sector, with appetite for this type of real estate illustrated by the sale of a 50% stake in their 1 Triton Square scheme to a large institutional investor at a 9% premium to book value in March 2024.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Charles Boissier on Castellum AB: We are Neutral on Castellum. Internal growth slowed considerably at the company’s Q1 24 results (+2.9% vs. +8.9% at FY 23), especially relative to peer Fabege (+9.0%). More generally, we note dividend reinstatement was a topic of discussion on the company’s call and in light of the financial situation of Castellum, we forecast dividend will remain suspended.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Wanda Serwinowska on Engie: We have a Buy rating on the stock. In our view, the reasons for Engie's historically low multiples are being addressed systematically, with: (i) volatile commodity-sensitive activities disposed; (ii) opaque energy services activities disposed; (iii) a nuclear exit pathway agreed in Belgium, with liabilities capped at last; (iv) a company-wide cost & simplification programme under way; (v) new multi-year earnings and dividend guidance issued; and (vi) a management focus on consistent delivery. The result should be a more stable and predictable business that no longer deserves the current discount to utility sector multiples, but track records take time to be established and this is likely to be a multi-year process

Wanda Serwinowska on E.ON: Our Buy rating is underpinned by: (i) lower risk (75% EBITDA is regulated); (ii) high exposure to accelerating grid spend and rising allowed returns; (iii) strong balance sheet relative to other grid operators such as Terna and National Grid. The stock trades at a discount to its peers and any move to risk-off or ratecutting scenarios in 2024 should add further upside, in our view.

## What our Chinese (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Nora Min on Fuyao Glass Industry Group (3606 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. The notable gross margin expansion in Q124 validated our view that Fuyao is one of the few auto parts companies that has strong bargaining power, endowed by its over 30% global market share. The importance of bargaining power could become more prominent amid intensified industry competition. We expect Fuyao to deliver robust earnings growth in 2024E, as: 1) binding with all mainstream OEMs, Fuyao’s customer structure is well balanced; 2) with rising contribution of high-value added product, such as all-glass roof, coating glass, and encapsulated glass, Fuyao’s ASP is likely to rise steadily.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Staples

Veronica Song on Want Want China Holdings (151 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We see attractiveness in WW's risk reward, considering its undemanding valuation vs peers, potential upside in final dividend and room for margin improvement.

Henry Liu on JD Health International (6618 HK): We upgraded JD Health’s rating from Neutral to Buy on May 11, as we believe investors may be overlooking a near-term sales growth recovery and a long-term trn Rmb market opportunity for JD Health. We expect potential sales growth recovery in Q2 to be a catalyst.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Financials

Alex Ye on Qifu Technology Inc (QFIN UW): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We see QFIN as a quality high-yield stock, with resilient earnings growth outlook (UBSe 2024 earnings +7%) and a total yield of 16% at current valuation (5% dividends + 11% buyback). We expect current buyback program of US\$350m over a 12 month horizon to be recurring, which provides good medium-term visibility on shareholders’ return.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Financials

Jessica Chan on New China Life Insurance (1336 HK): We have a Neutral rating on NCI. It is indeed a less preferred name in the insurance space because 1) NCI has a relatively high value of new business (VNB) sensitivity to investment return. The interest rate environment in China is still subject to uncertainty, 2) Agency productivity is low relative to listed peers, meaning that its growth is more headcount-driven. We highlight the massive recruitment model has become less effective, due to the diminishing demographic dividend, and 3) Business quality is not ideal vs peers, as evidenced by its relatively low persistency ratio.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Anita Wei on Shanghai Pharmaceuticals (2607 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. We like Shanghai Pharma for its differentiated one-stop distribution services and sales potential of self-produced novel drug pipeline, and forecast revenue/earnings CAGRs of 10%/11% during 2024-2026E. We are optimistic that leading distribution players could benefit from the industry consolidation and being the beneficiary of SOE reforms.

Chen Chen on Akeso Inc (9926 HK): We have a Buy rating on Akeso. We expect key regulatory catalysts in 2024/2025E: 1) 5 (s)NDA approvals including cadonilimab (in 1L GC/GEJC, end 2024E and 1L CC, H125E), ivonescimab (in 2L nsqNSCLC, Q224E), ebronucimab (PCSK9 in hypercholesterolemia, H224E), ebdarokimab (IL-12/IL-23 in plaque psoriasis, H224/H125E) and penpulimab (PD-1 in 1L NPC, H224/H125E); 2) sNDA submission of AK112 in 1L NSCLC (PD-L1+) in H224E, and Gumokimab (IL-17A, AK111, NDA in psoriasis) in 2024E/early 2025E.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Health Care

Chen Chen on Wuxi Bio (2269 HK) and Wuxi Apptec (2359 HK): We think the 2032 clause is marginally positive in the short to mid term as companies in the scope may have a better chance to source early-stage project orders. However, given the limited visibility of the final outcome and the final version of the bills, we maintain our Neutral ratings.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Perry Yeung on BOC Aviation Ltd (2588 HK): We have a Buy rating on BOC. We expect continuous recovery in net lease yield in 2024 and beyond. While the high interest rate environment in the near term could continue to present upward pressure on the cost of debt, UBS's US economics team forecasts significantly lower US interest rates in 2024E. At the same time, robust airline earnings should support fleet expansion, while the slow delivery rate of new aircraft could prompt airlines to source new aircraft from lessors, translating to stronger leasing demand and lease rate. In FY23 result, the company reported a better-than-expected capex, which should be supportive of a higher lease rental income in FY24 with bigger operating asset base.

Phyllis Wang on CRRC Corp (1766 HK): CRRC-H is one of our top picks due to improved earnings outlook, attractive valuation (10.5x/8.9x 2024E/25E PE) & 5-6% estimated div yields. We are positive on rolling stock industry in 2024-25 due to healthy passenger traffic growth, solid growth of maintenance service and potential accelerating of locomotive replacement.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Materials

Amily Guo on Dongyue Group (189 HK): We have a Buy rating on Dongyue. We expect PVDF prices to stabilize as the price level has already fallen to historical lows. And we expect third-generation (3rd-gen) refrigerant business to drive company's earnings recovery as we forecast strong refrigerant prices in 2024-25.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Materials

Xinlei Li on China cement names: We expect China cement utilization to continue to slide 1-2ppt in 2024-25E due to sustained overcapacity situation. We expect cement profitability to hover at current low level as demand remains lackluster and inventory has built up to historical high level.

## What our Japanese (fundamental) sector analysts think the highest/ lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Kohei Takahashi on Denso Corp: We have a Buy rating on Denso. Winner of HEVs which continues to gain market share in the US. New vehicle software business for Toyota will create new revenue stream in the next 3 years.

Kohei Takahashi on Suzuki Motor Corp: We have a Buy rating on Suzuki. Sales growth and mix improvement in India should continue to drive its earnings growth. HEVs are growing rapidly even in India, and Suzuki benefits fully from lineup of HEVs by utilizing the alliance with Toyota.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Consumer Discretionary

Kohei Takahashi on Nissan Motor Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Nissan. It aims to grow aggressively in the US which will potentially lower margin by expanding incentives. Expanding investments on BEV will lower its margin structurally.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Kikkoman Corp: We have a Buy rating on Kikkoman because 1) we see steady topline growth in US and EU soysauce, 2) we can expect the topline recovery of wholesaler business going forward, and 3) still the multiple is lower than the past 5 years average.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Rei Ihara on Meiji Holdings Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Meiji because 1) it released aggressive guidance, unclear message to market, 2) it already released buyback, but no potential to do more in short term, and 3) continued weakness of topline in food business.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Kasumi Haruta on Shionogi & Co Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Focus is on S-309309 obesity drug phase 2 data read out which is scheduled on June 7th. If successful, obesity is a huge market and it could be one of Shionogi's next drivers.

Tomoko Yoshihara on Terumo Corp: We have a Buy rating on Terumo because 1) US business should remain brisk due to the market recovery and new products, and 2) the rollout of plasma source business is taking place slightly earlier that the initial plan.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Kasumi Haruta on Eisai Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Eisai. Alzheimer's drug Leqembi ramp up should be still slow and we think it is likely to miss the guidance. Focus is on Leqembi, if it doesn't become a pillar for the company, overcoming patent cliff should be difficult going forward.

Tomoko Yoshihara on M3 Inc: We are Neutral on M3 because 1) there should be limited recovery at pharmaceutical marketing services, and 2) the restructuring is under way at US clinical trial business.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Industrials

Perry Yeung on Ana Holdings Inc: We are Neutral on Ana. The risk reward appears balanced rather than skewing to the upside, in our view. On the positive side, travel demand remains strong, which should bode well for revenue at least in the coming quarters. However, we would flag the risk of potential cost inflation (as seen in the recent quarter) and potential traffic share loss to JAL due to GTF engine issue.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Industrials

Kohei Takahashi on Hino Motors: We are Neutral on Hino. ASEAN Market, key growth driver, will continue to decline. The total cost of misconduct in the US was not fixed yet, which adds risk of delaying a merger with Fuso.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Info Tech

Shunta Omura on Sumco Corp: We have a Sell rating on Sumco. We forecast supply/ demand imbalance of 12” wafers. Short term wafer demand recovery is also dull.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Materials

Shunta Omura on Asahi Kasei Corp: We are Neutral on the stock. We see stronger growth from housing and healthcare business, and potential portfolio improvement from material business.

Shunta Omura on Toray Industries Inc: We have Buy rating on Toray. We start to see management change more favorably to enhance capital efficiency.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Materials

Shunta Omura on Sumitomo Chemical Co Ltd: We have a Sell rating on the stock. Fundamentally, refinery and petrochemical business, pharma business continues to struggle, with no clear growth from other businesses.

## What our Australian (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Shaun Cousins on Harvey Norman Holdings: Retain Buy as Australian Franchisees revenue positioned for growth and Franchising Ops PBT margin outlook improving, while there is a low level of crowding, high short interest, and UBSe earnings well above mkt.

Tim Piper on Eagers Automotive Ltd: We are Neutral on the stock. New car demand remains relatively resilient in a higher interest rate environment, though has become patchier more recently. We like the consolidation story for APE but remain wary of a normalisation in new car gross margin,s which remains close to record highs and well above pre COVID levels, with feedback suggesting margins have now begun declining on higher inventory.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Apoorv Sehgal on Graincorp Ltd: Buy based on through-the-cycle earnings and scope for capital returns. We expect a likely working capital unwind over FY24E (as crop conditions normalise in dryer conditions) to support a net core cash position expanding from \$200m at 1H23 to \$460m by FY24E, or 30% of mcap, before likely \~\$104m of proceeds from sale of its 8.5% UMG stake. We expect GNC to return a portion of this cash to shareholders. Stock trading on \~5x through-the-cycle EBITDA looks relatively cheap.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Energy

Tom Allen on Santos Ltd: We have a Buy rating on Santos (STO.asx). It is our most preferred Australian Energy exposure (Buy, \$9/sh). Trading at 5x 2025e EV/EBITDA and an implied price of 65/bbl indicates good value, considering that we expect STO to grow production at 9% CAGR over 2024-27e as the Barossa gas and Pikka oil projects deliver first flows from 3Q25 and early 2026, respectively. This step change in production drives a commensurate lift (8% CAGR over 2024-27e) in OCF lifting STO to a sustainable div yield at \~6.5% while investing in new growth.

Tom Allen on Beach Energy Ltd: We have a Buy rating on the stock. Beach Energy (BPT.asx) is our second preferred Australian Energy exposure (Buy, \$1.85/sh) and is trading at an implied price of \$69/bbl, however screens inexpensive at 3.4x 2025e EV/ EBITDA. We forecast Beach Energy’s production to grow at 24% CAGR over FY24-26e as Waitsia Stage 2 project in the onshore Perth basin (WA) and new production from the Thylacine West and Enterprise developments in the offshore VIC Otways ramp up in FY25e. These projects drive BPT from a 12% to 22% FCF yield from FY25-26e and a 6% to 11% div yield over the same timeframe. We believe the new CEO has the FCF and strong balance sheet to support both strong shareholder returns and to add new medium term production growth.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Financials

Scott Russell on Medibank Private Ltd: We have a Buy rating on Medibank because 1) resident claims inflation remains low, and recently confirmed “low end” of 2.2-2.4% guidance for this year. This is supporting high margins and high retention rates. UBSe forecasts are above consensus, 2) Australia’s health insurance industry is enjoying persistent tailwinds from COVID – higher margins and higher policy growth, and 3) nonresident segment is recovering quickly as visa numbers surge and margins recover post COVID.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Financials

Scott Russell on ASX Ltd: We have a Sell rating on ASX because 1) consensus opex and capex expectations are likely too low as the company works to upgrade CHESS system over the next 5 years, 2) EBIT margins are high vs global Exchange peers, and we believe will continue to decline as competitive threat becomes clearer, and 3) stock expensive at 26x vs global peers and vs 10Y average below 20x.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Health Care

Laura Sutcliffe on Telix Pharmaceuticals Ltd: Top pick in Australian healthcare – high growth, CF positive biotech with imminent pipeline developments.

Laura Sutcliffe on CSL Ltd: We see upside risk to 2-3yr earnings estimates as delivery on components of gross margin recovery materialises.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Industrials

Nathan Reilly on ALS Ltd: We are Neutral rated on ALS with the stock trading at an FY25E EV/EBITDA of 10.5x (+1% premium to global TIC peers), and with FY24/25E EPS growth of -3%/+2% (UBSe). We see the turnaround at Nuvisan, integration of recent acquisitions, and a recovery in exploration activity as key catalysts for the stock into FY25.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Info Tech

Apoorv Sehgal on Technology One: We are Neutral on the stock. We view TNE as a high quality stock, but the company’s medium-term financial targets (ARR and PBT margins) look priced in and the stock is trading on a rich multiple, in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Levi Spry on Bellevue Gold Ltd: Quality and value. We like it a lot, good growth and asset.

Lachlan Shaw on South32 Ltd: Improving outlook after poor last 12 months, aluminium business strength.

Nathan Reilly on Incitec Pivot Ltd: On IPL, we’re Buy rated with a \$3.21 price target. The share price has performed well YTD and is up 10%. We continue to see valuation support in the name, with the stock trading at a FY24E FCF yield of 9% (10yr ave. 8%) and a 15% disc. to BV.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Levi Spry on Regis Resources Ltd and Pilbara Minerals Ltd: We have Sell ratings on both RRL and PLS. PLS has quality businesses, but like RRL, it is trading very expensive and beyond fundamentals, in our view.

Nathan Reilly on Orora Ltd: We are Neutral on Orora. The company recently downgraded its FY24 earnings guidance on weaker performance from both the Saverglass and NA Distribution businesses. Looking beyond FY24, we think investors are focused on the potential for Saverglass to add a third growth platform to Orora's existing ANZ beverage and North American distribution units, however at this point, the stock trades at a 1yr fwd P/E of 13x (vs. LT ave 17x), offering FY24E/25E EPS growth of -30%/0%. The company's leverage guidance of 2.8x at FY24 is elevated and increasingly in focus given the uncertain earnings growth outlook.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Real Estate

Tom Bodor on Arena Reit: We are Buy rated on the stock. ARF’s portfolio provides resilient income in a real estate subsector exposed to favourable demographic tailwinds (childcare) while also offering upside through the development of new facilities.

Tom Bodor on GPT Group: We are Buy rated on the stock. GPT has a diverse portfolio with retail and logistics assets that continue to perform strongly and a well-regarded funds management platform. Soft performance in the office portfolio remains a focus, although the recent CEO transition provides scope for an updated strategy to address areas of weakness and unlock value.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Real Estate

Tom Bodor on Lifestyle Communities Ltd: Recent capital raising and downgrades have been reflected in the share price (hence our recent upgrade to Neutral) but the fundamental issue remains the weak Victorian residential market (to which LIC has 100% exposure).

Tom Bodor on Charter Hall Retail Reit: We are Neutral on the stock. CQR's portfolio income growth continues to benefit from increased exposure to long WALE convenience retail assets with CPI linked income and triple net leases; however, this isn't enough to offset debt cost headwinds, in our view.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Utilities

Tom Allen on Origin Energy Ltd: Origin Energy (ORG.asx) is our most preferred exposure in Australian Utilities (Buy, \$10.55/sh). Trading at 5.2x FY24/25 EV EBITDA the stock remains inexpensive (relative to NZ and EMEA integrated electric utilities) and multiple levers to support outperformance. Origin Energy has the largest fleet of peaking generation assets in Australia’s National Electricity Market, leaving it well placed to benefit from growing intra-day price spreads. We forecast Energy Markets EBITDA to see an 8% CAGR over FY25e-27e as new earnings are added from x4 new utility scale batteries over that timeframe and a supportive electricity & gas pricing outlook. We expect cashflows from ORG’s 27.5% interest in APLNG to support Origin’s energy transition spend and dividend profile which we expect can sustainably support a \~6% div yield with upside risk of higher returns as Origin Energy introduces a new distribution policy at an Investor Briefing Day scheduled for 12 June 2024.

Tom Allen on AGL Energy Ltd: We are Buy rated (\$11.25/sh) on AGL (AGL.asx) and currently trading at 4.8x FY25e EBITDA, the stocks screens very inexpensive at >20% discount to its avg historical multiple despite a strong earnings outlook. We recognise that AGL has the lowest cost generation portfolio of the major integrated utilities in Australia, and as higher cost thermal generating assets exit the market over the next 10 years, expect AGL to outperform. We forecast underlying NPAT 7% and 18% ahead of consensus over FY25-26e and recognising that 2025 electricity futures prices have stepped materially higher in recent weeks, expect consensus upgrades to follow in the coming months.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Utilities

Tom Allen on APA Group: APA Group (APA.asx) is our least preferred exposure in the Australian Utilities sector. Trading at 12.4 and 11.4x FY24/25e EBITDA, APA appears expensive relative to US and EMEA peers (at 9-10x) and its div yield offers only a 1.3% spread to the Aust 10yr Gov bond yield. We remain cautious of balance sheet pressure with FFO /net debt at 9.9% in Feb-24 and expected to dip below S&P’s BBB downward rating threshold over the coming year. As APA seeks to expand its exposure to electrification (diversifying away from its heavy gas exposure) we see numerous growth opportunities albeit offering modest returns and heavy upfront greenfield capex. Further, growing regulatory pressures could add downside risk not yet factored into the price.

## All regions in one chart

Figure 151: Price momentum keeps improving for Chinese stocks. Info Tech and Communication Services momentums have started to fade recently, although still positive. Overall, the most favoured sectors are Energy, Financials & Materials, the least favoured are Real Estate, Communication Services & Health Care
![](images/4619c1c29af2977d3f6d5a6c3861ed160ed95b1b54a9b947d12088878185e3b7.jpg)

![](images/e2bca1263ab7f83ceaf51e7ae1de5568ab688675c1103779469c1344c84322c8.jpg)

Figure 152: Positioning remains very supportive in China, while being more balanced in other regions. In US and Europe, Real estate is clearly the sector to overweight based on positioning. In the other regions, the top pick is consistently Info Tech. Positioning in Health Care stocks has significantly improved vs. March, as investors may start to feel a bit nervous/ more defensive after a strong start of the year

![](images/1a7deed475ebb1c3a1d782b704a9c29d2db839af4159bf9402791a259e9ab38f.jpg)

Figure 154: With OECD G20 Composite Leading index indicating expansion as the most likely regime in 2024 (43% probability, vs. 12% for a downturn, and 3% higher than previous update), cyclical sectors continue to be favoured in our regime model

![](images/b69c978e513cbe6459a1fb7d1d6ceef791a23dd6ce22e429a59493c8b5b59560.jpg)

Figure 155: On valuation, Financials & Utilities remain the cheapest sectors in our adjusted 12mth fwd PE model, when Tech & Health Care remain the most expensive. Since the last update, relative valuations have slightly improved in Consumer Discretionary & Financials, and slightly worsened in Utilities & Materials

Figure 157: When all indicators are aggregated, the model clearly displays a risk-on stance. Cyclical sectors - Industrials, Financials, Materials - are favoured, when defensive sectors - Health Care and Staples - exhibit lower allocations. ## Valuation Method and Risk Statement

Risks of multi-asset investing include but are not limited to market risk, credit risk, interest rate risk, and foreign exchange risk. Correlations of returns among different asset classes may deviate from historical patterns. Geopolitical events and policy shocks pose risks that can reduce asset returns. Valuations may be adversely affected during times of high market volatility, thin liquidity, and economic dislocation.

KEY DEFINITIONS: Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Short-Term Ratings reflect the expected near-term (up to three months) performance of the stock and do not reflect any change in the fundamental view or investment case. Equity Price Targets have an investment horizon of 12 months.

EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES: UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy: Positive on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core Banding Exceptions (CBE): Exceptions to the standard +/-6% bands may be granted by the Investment Review Committee (IRC). Factors considered by the IRC include the stock's volatility and the credit spread of the respective company's debt. As a result, stocks deemed to be very high or low risk may be subject to higher or lower bands as they relate to the rating. When such exceptions apply, they will be identified in the Company Disclosures table in the relevant research piece.

## Company profile and fee and risk statement under the Japanese Financial Instruments & Exchange Law

Company Name etc: UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd., Financial Instruments & Exchange Firm, Kanto Local Financial Bureau (Kinsho) No.2633

Associated Memberships: Japan Securities Dealers' Association, the Financial Futures Association of Japan, and Type II Financial Instruments Firms Association

UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. will receive a brokerage fee based on an individual contract and no standard upper limit or calculating method. For the trading of domestic stocks, consumption tax is added to the fee. For the trading of foreign stock, fee on the foreign stock exchange or foreign tax may be charged in addition to the domestic fee and tax. Those amounts may vary depending on the jurisdiction. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the stock in the case of trading stocks, and that a loss may occur due to the exchange rate in the case of trading foreign stocks. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price or performance of the properties in the portfolio in the case of trading REITs

UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. will only receive the purchasing amounts for trading unlisted bonds (JGBs, municipals, government guaranteed bonds, corporate bonds) when UBS Securities Japan Co., Ltd. is the counterparty. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the bond caused by the fluctuations in the interest rates, and that a loss may occur due to the exchange rate in the case of trading foreign bonds.

For the detailed information of commissions and risks associated with individual financial instrument transactions, please carefully read the documents provided prior to the execution of the contracts or other materials for customers, etc. before executing any transaction.
